Propose at most 3 distinct optimization hypotheses. First write one clear 80–150 word statement per idea, then specify its test. Try mechanism transfer, constraint-specific adaptation, and resolving conflicting evidence. Do not fill the quota with weak ideas. Do not invent measured bottlenecks or numerical speedups. Separate predictions from observations. Include a strong existing/static comparator, competing explanation, held-out workload, and support/reject/inconclusive criteria. Use concise mechanism-based prior_art_query (2–5 terms), not every hardware keyword. IDs must be unique in this run. Parents must name real earlier IDs; revise after objections rather than rename. Optimize usefulness against this baseline under the supplied task rubric. Known methods, flags and ports are allowed. Novelty is descriptive only unless the rubric explicitly enables it. Historical rejections are context, not bans. If baseline inclusion is unknown, propose a cheap presence/eligibility check as the first step. Parent IDs may also use global hypothesis IDs from memory. Return zero ideas if evidence cannot support any.

of\n#41589\n(DeepSeek-V4.1 prefill optimizations, split into smaller PRs).\nMotivation\nThe low-ratio (ratio 1/2) prefill indexer ran many small kernels per layer, sorted and remapped its selection before attention, had a schedule that is quadratic in a request's rows, and synchronized with the host several times per step.\nModifications\nFused index-Q kernel in the prefill indexer\n: prefill query and head-weight prep goes through\nindex_q_rope_pack_weights\n(\n_index_q_and_weights\n), shared with decode.\nPrefill top-k written straight into the raw selection\n: the DeepGEMM prefill top-k emits request-local compressed positions directly into the buffer sparse prefill reads. Pool slots (\npage_indices\n) are only derived when this forward's attention reads them (trtllm, non-sparse extend, sm120, late-layer tail, request window, or\nSGLANG_OPT_FLASHMLA_SPARSE_PREFILL\noff).\nSelection\nis removed; the output buffers are fields of\nPrefillInputs\n/\nDecodeInputs\n/\nCapturedPrefillInputs\n.\nwrite_selection\nkeeps only the CP dense-blocks path and no longer sorts.\nRow-pair request ids for the paged sparse indexer schedule\n: DeepGEMM's paged sparse-logits metadata walks back over equal request ids once per row. Ids unique per consecutive row pair of a request (never spanning two requests) bound the walk to one step; logits are unchanged.\nNo host syncs in the prefill forward\n:\nget_deep_gemm_prefill_data\nbuilds the flattened-K layout for the whole batch from host lengths instead of a per-request loop indexing with device scalars.\nNew\nasync_h2d\nin\nsrt/utils/common.py\n(pinned, non-blocking; the counterpart of\nasync_d2h\n), used for the request starts, row-pair ids, CP rows, tail rows, and the engram history.\nEngram's\nrepeat_interleave\ntakes\noutput_size\nfrom\nextend_seq_lens_cpu\n.\nNo\nrecord_stream\non the mHC stats input\n: the stats kernels read the residual on the side stream, and every consumer joins that stream on the main stream while the residual is still referenced, so\nrecord_stream\nonly delayed the reuse of each freed residual. Without the host syncs the host runs further ahead, and those delayed frees piled up to several full residuals and exhausted the allocator.\nTests\n:\ntest_dense_prefill_indexer.py\nand\ntest_dsv4_nonpaged_indexer.py\nfollow the new interfaces.\nChecklist / TODO\nAdd coverage for the row-pair ids and the batched flattened-K layout.\n\ud83e\udd16 Generated with\nClaude Code\nCI States\nLatest PR Test (Base): \u274c\nRun #37101259115\nLatest PR Test (Extra): \u274c\nRun #37101259025\nLatest PR Test (AMD ROCm 10): \u274c\nRun #37101259187\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\nDarkSharpness\nand others\nadded\n5\ncommits\nSeptember 29, 2026 01:35\n[DSv4.1] Use the fused index-Q kernel in the prefill indexer\n\u2026\nff231c1\nPrefill now shares decode's index_q_rope_pack_weights: RoPE, both fp4\nroundings, the pack and head weights in one launch instead of the Triton\nrope fake-quant, a second quantization and two elementwise ops\n(~220 us -> ~60 us per index layer at 8192 tokens on B200).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n[DSv4.1] Write the prefill top-k straight into the raw selection\n\u2026\n9fde803\nThe DeepGEMM prefill top-k now writes request-local compressed positions\ninto the raw-index buffer that sparse prefill reads, instead of flattened\ncolumns that write_selection sorted and mapped back. plumbing they expose:\nmHC sequence-parallel boundary\n(\nSGLANG_OPT_DSV41_MHC_SP_FUSION\n, opt-in): inside the sharded region each sublayer boundary becomes one collective launch \u2014 reduce-scatter of the partial sublayer output, hc post + next combine + RMSNorm on this rank's rows, all-gather of the normalized next input \u2014 with the mixing statistics produced by dedicated CTAs of the same launch (NVLS from three peers up, peer load/store at two ranks). With decoder SWA bounded replay the SP region ends at the tail boundary instead of excluding SP outright.\nEngram\n: the hash tables shard on hash-column boundaries in symmetric memory, and the lookup defaults to an in-place gather straight into\nwkv\n's MXFP8 operand \u2014 no all-reduce, no dequant/requant round trip (a bf16-dequantizing gather covers\nwkv\nconfigurations without an MXFP8 input path, exactly; the shared host table is read the same way). The gate + residual update + the next boundary's combine/norm fuse into one seam kernel with SP / DP / TP variants (\nSGLANG_OPT_DSV41_ENGRAM_TP_FUSION_MIN_TOKENS\ngates the TP variant; the image-token mask rides the kernel as a per-row skip). CUDA only; ROCm and other platforms keep the existing paths.\nMoE\n: a raw\nMoEOutput\n's finalize now defers whenever the producer supports it and lands as one fused finalize + shared-add kernel writing the caller's buffer;\nget_merged\nhonors\nout=\non the unfused arm too, fixing a path where the sequence-parallel boundary read an unwritten buffer.\nAccuracy\nKernel level: the direct gathers are bitwise-identical to the dequantizing reference (including e8m0 zero scales and fp8 denormal payloads) on both sm90 and sm100; the DP and SP seam variants are bitwise-identical to each other across row counts, ragged splits and skip masks, including NaN/Inf injection into masked rows; 200 back-to-back SP launches show stable semaphore rotation.\nEnd to end (TP4/EP4, gsm8k 1319 questions, greedy): fused SP 0.9014 vs unfused 0.9113, McNemar p = 0.198 (not significant). A same-configuration rerun control scores 0.9113 / 0.9113 with only 36% exact-text agreement, i.e. the engine is not run-to-run stable at temperature 0 on this backend (as the cookbook documents), and the fused path's long-prompt logprob drift sits within that noise floor. TP-mode fusion scores 0.9052; TP2 (host table) 0.8954, all within the observed jitter band.\nThe seam consumes the previous boundary's lagged\npre\n, so the fused math matches the unfused operator order; equivalence rather than bitwise identity is expected against the old path (the gate's sigmoid was already approximate there).\nNotes\nTP2 cannot hold the device tables beside the weights; the host table is the deployable form there (cookbook updated, along with the gather semantics).\nOnline weight updates remain rejected while derived hc weight splits are active; the probe follows the renamed stacked tensors.\n\ud83e\udd16 Generated with\nClaude Code\nCI States\nLatest PR Test (Base): \u274c\nRun #38288421172\nLatest PR Test (Extra): \u274c\nRun #38288420820\nLatest PR Test (AMD ROCm 10): \u274c\nRun #38288421125\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\nmintlify\nBot\ncommented\nOct 8, 2026\n\u2022\nedited\nLoading\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nContributor\nPreview deployment for your docs. 4x-inflated shape under EP, and the obvious EP-aware fix regresses serving\n#40417\nCopy link\nDescription\nLeoyzen\nopened\non Sep 20, 2026\nIssue body actions\nDescribe the bug\nestimate_local_valid_shape_m()\nin\npython/sglang/srt/layers/moe/moe_runner/humming.py\nreturns\ntopk_ids.nelement()\nwhen no\nexpected_m\nis given (~line 204, carries\n# TODO: update for EP and DP\n, introduced in\n#23754\n).\nUnder EP this counts the global routed elements.\nlocal_expert_mapping\nkeeps the tensor shape unchanged and writes -1 for non-local experts, so\nnelement()\n= M_global x top_k, while each rank's GEMM only touches ~1/ep_size of those rows.\nThe value only drives kernel config selection: the Python block-size ladder in\n_prepare_indexed_gemm_kwargs\nand the humming launcher's variant lookup (\nfind_kernel_launch_data\nin launcher.cpp). Correctness is unaffected, the launcher validates against real tensor shapes. So under EP every humming MoE GEMM picks its config for a workload ~4x larger than what the rank actually computes (EP4).\nWe tried the obvious fix, a graph-safe host estimate\nmax(1, M * top_k / ep_size)\n(exact counting needs a D2H sync, illegal under CUDA graph capture). It moves every working point to the true local shape and passes unit tests, but serving regressed:\narm\ndecode tok/s/req\nvs parent\nparent (current code)\n52.2\n-\nEP-aware estimate\n49.5\n-5.1% (perm p=0.003, n=10/arm)\nEP-aware + ladder bands recalibrated from per-point kernel microbench\n-\n-4.0% e2e (C48 median, 3-arm check)\nSetup: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (384 routed experts, top_k 6, MXFP4 experts), TP4/EP4, 4x H200-class GPUs, DSPark speculative decoding, random workload at 32-48 concurrency, unique-seed A/B protocol with flush_cache between rounds, cross-session reproducible.\nPer-cubin enumeration against the deployed heuristic ladder explains why. The ladder bands were calibrated against the global-element scale, and the two gemm types disagree about what the input even means:\nindexed, routed rows ~750-2100: the local-rows input picks kernels 22-26% faster than the global-scale input\ngrouped, same points: opposite direction, the global-scale input wins by 8-12%\nsmall decode CUDA-graph tiers (<200 rows): both land in the same band, zero effect\nre-mapping ladder bands empirically flipped kernel rankings once real routing skew and CUDA-graph replay entered the picture\nA single scalar cannot carry both total routed rows and per-expert density once num_local_experts != num_experts. For reference, the masked/DeepEP-LL path already uses\nexpected_m * num_local_experts\n, and DeepEP-normal dispatch carries exact host-side\nnum_recv_tokens_per_expert\n.\nReproduction\nAny EP>1 run with\n--moe-runner-backend humming\n(default gemm type indexed). The wrong value is observable without serving, it is just\ntopk_ids.nelement()\nunder EP:\n# decode bs=48, top_k=6, EP4: topk_ids (48, 6).nelement() = 288 passed as\n# valid_shape_m while the rank's GEMM only handles ~72 rows.\n# See estimate_local_valid_shape_m in\n# python/sglang/srt/layers/moe/moe_runner/humming.py\nServing A/B protocol:\npython3 -m sglang.benchmark.serving --dataset-name random --random-input-len 1024 --random-output-len 256 --random-range-ratio 1 --num-prompts 384 --max-concurrency 48\n, unique seed per round, /flush_cache between rounds, drop first warmup round, median of 4.\nEnvironment\nPython: 3.12.3, PyTorch: 2.13.0+cu130, CUDA: 13.0 (driver 580.105.08)\nGPU 0-3: NVIDIA L20X (SM90)\nsglang: 0.0.0.dev1+g20518d851, sglang-kernel: 0.4.7\nflashinfer: 0.6.18, triton: 3.7.1, transformers: 5.12.1\nPossible fixes (for discussion)\n(a) Document\nvalid_shape_m\nas a hint defined on the global routed-element scale and leave the estimator as-is (what we reverted to; zero risk).\n(b) Make ladder generation convention-aware per gemm_type and re-tune per device.\n(c) Extend the launcher/config-selection API to take (rows, local expert count) or tokens-per-expert as a second key.\nHappy to share the full A/B matrix and per-cubin timing data, attaching below.\nReactions are currently unavailable\nActivity\nLeoyzen\ncommented\non\nSep 20, 2026\nLeoyzen\non Sep 20, 2026\nContributor\nAuthor\nMore actions\nRaw data for the numbers above (A/B bench JSONs, per-cubin microbench results, 3-arm C48 check):\nhttps://github.com/Leoyzen/sglang/tree/evidence/issue-40417-humming-ep\nSign up for free\nto join this conversation on GitHub.\nAlready have an account?\nSign in to comment\nMetadata\nMetadata\nAssignees\nNo one assigned\nLabels\nNo labels\nNo labels\nType\nNo type\nProjects\nNo projects\nMilestone\nNo milestone\nRelationships\nNone yet\nDevelopment\nNo branches or pull requests\nIssue actions\nOpen in GitHub Copilot app", "created_at": null, "updated_at": null, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:23:32Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:sgl-project/sglang:40417", "tags": ["deepseek-v4.1-flash", "deepseek", "moe", "throughput", "speculation", "communication"], "metadata": {"id": "github:sgl-project/sglang:40417", "repo": "sgl-project/sglang", "number": 40417, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:23:31Z", "discussion_coverage": "HTML-visible only; Otherwise, it will be closed.\nDescribe the bug\nServing\ndeepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash\nwith\n--speculative-algorithm DSPARK\non\n2 nodes \u00d7 4 H200 (TP8 + EP8, LeaderWorkerSet, one 4-GPU pod per node over RoCE)\ndeterministically deadlocks during startup, right after the DSpark draft weights finish loading. With this change, the next token's hidden data, scale factors, metadata, and routing information are prefetched while previous remote stores remain in flight.\nAt steady state, the three buffers are used for:\nA previous token whose remote S2G store may still be in flight.\nThe current token being sent to remote ranks.\nThe next token being prefetched from global memory.\nBefore recycling the oldest buffer,\ntma_store_wait<1>()\nreduces the number of outstanding store groups to one. This keeps buffer reuse safe without fully draining all remote stores between tokens.\nThe shared-memory calculation now accounts for all three staging buffers, and direct NVLink dispatch is limited to at most 14 dispatch warps. It improves the existing Hopper W4 grouped GEMM paths and adds a new\nMXFP4 weight + FP8 activation path:\nMXFP4xBF16: optimize the existing MXFP4 weight + BF16 activation path.\nINT4xFP8: optimize the existing INT4 weight + FP8 activation path.\nMXFP4xFP8: add MXFP4 weight + FP8 activation with pre-MMA E8M0 scale fusion and runtime\nper-token FP8 activation quantization.\nThe MXFP4xFP8 path follows the high-level weight preprocessing and\ndequantization idea used by\nHumming\n:\nthe MXFP4 weight scale is transformed into a bounded E8M0 exponent offset and\nfolded into the FP8 operand before MMA. The main changes are:\nEnable Hopper FP4 kernels without requiring CUDA native FP4 headers on SM90\nby using CUTLASS subbyte FP4 types.\nAdd optimized mixed-input mainloops for post-MMA scaling and pre-MMA E8M0\nscale fusion.\nPrecompute grouped GEMM work scheduling and TMA descriptor state before the\nmain GEMM kernel.\nUse folded weight-scale storage so the weight-scale layout is independent of\nthe profiled\nTileK\n.\nAdd precomputed scheduler kernels for ping-pong and cooperative grouped GEMM\nschedulers.\nAdd runtime activation expansion and per-token FP8 quantization support for\nthe pre-MMA E8M0 scale-fusion path.\nAdd per-token scale epilogue callbacks for the FP8 activation + MXFP4 weight\npath.\nExtend autotune and tactic validation so Hopper mixed-input tactics are\nchecked for correctness before being selected.\nAdd and update tests for Hopper MXFP4xBF16, INT4xFP8, and MXFP4xFP8 pre-MMA scale\npaths.\n\ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\nN/A\nPerformance\nBenchmarks were run on NVIDIA H20 and H200 GPUs using MoE workload configurations matching DeepSeek-V3 (256 experts, top-8, hidden size 7168, intermediate size 2048) and DeepSeek-V4-Pro (384 experts, top-6, hidden size 7168, intermediate size 3072).\nhttps://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V3\nhttps://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro\nThe plots below summarize the PR performance results. Positive values mean FlashInfer is faster.\n|\nDS4 FlashInfer Humming GEMM speedup vs upstream Humming\nImplementation Notes\nThe Hopper FP4 compatibility path uses CUTLASS FP4 storage on SM90 and keeps\nCUDA native FP4 as a toolkit-dependent feature instead of a Hopper\nrequirement.\nThe mixed-input grouped GEMM scheduler now supports a precomputed work map.\nThis avoids doing grouped tile lookup and descriptor updates in the main GEMM\nhot path.\nThe folded weight-scale layout decouples model preprocessing from the\nselected\nTileK\n, which lets autotune choose among multiple\nTileK\nvalues\nwithout requiring multiple prepacked weight-scale layouts.\nThe pre-MMA E8M0 scale path fuses the weight scale into FP8 operands before\nWGMMA and applies the activation token scale in the epilogue.\nThe MXFP4xFP8 pre-MMA scale path is inspired by Humming's weight\npreprocessing/dequantization flow:\nhttps://github.com/inclusionAI/humming\nThe runtime FP8 activation quantization path computes per-token activation\nscales while expanding routed MoE rows.\nThe autotune validation path now rejects tactics that fail correctness before\ntiming and selection.\n\ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\nThank you for contributing to FlashInfer! One source of truth for the dispatch decision, the\n    compile key and the launch, which must agree.\n    \"\"\"\n    if num_tokens > _FUSED_POST_PRE_MAX_TOKENS:\n        return None\n    n_thr =
